---

## Complete UI/UX Brainstorm — DigitalMyHotels 2026

### 1. Typography — The Most Impactful Single Change

**Research verdict:**

- **Inter** (current): Safe, ubiquitous, best for dense data tables, tabular numerals built-in. But "looks competent, never distinctive."
- **Geist**: Better for technical/developer tools. Vercel's own font.
- **Plus Jakarta Sans**: Warmest of the three — humanist geometry, softer terminals. Used by HR tech, hospitality, fintech. Perfect brand match for a *hotel* platform.

**Recommendation for DigitalMyHotels:**

```
Headings + UI labels:  Plus Jakarta Sans  (warmth, hospitality feel)
Body text + tables:    Inter              (precision, dense data)
Numbers/financials:    Inter with tabular-nums  (₹13,245 aligned perfectly)
```

Why this pairing works: Hotel ops is both human (guests, staff) AND precise (money, dates). The split font system reflects both personalities. This is exactly how top SaaS products like Stripe (their own + body) and Linear (Inter Display + Inter) work.

---

### 2. Icon System — What to Change and What to Keep

**Lucide is the right call. Keep it.** Research confirms it is the best for clean SaaS UI, best React/shadcn compatibility, tree-shakeable. Switching to Tabler or Phosphor mid-project is a massive risk with no meaningful gain.

**What we CAN do better with Lucide:**

- Use `strokeWidth` as a design tool: `1.5` for dense table actions, `2` standard, `2.25` for prominent featured icons
- Scale consistently: `size-3.5` micro → `size-4` standard → `size-5` prominent → `size-6` illustrated
- Colored icons contextually: gold for financial, info-blue for information, status colors for statuses

---

### 3. The Glass Layer — Where and Where NOT

**Apple's fundamental rule from iOS 26 Liquid Glass research:**

> "Liquid Glass is exclusively for the **navigation layer** that floats above app content. Never apply to content itself (lists, tables, media)."

This applies perfectly to web SaaS. Here's the exact map:

```
GLASS LAYER (translucent, blurred):
  ✅ Top header bar        → sticky + backdrop-blur
  ✅ Sidebar               → subtle translucency  
  ✅ Modals + dialogs      → frosted glass
  ✅ Floating dropdowns    → glass surface
  ✅ Notification panel    → glass surface
  ✅ Mobile bottom sheet   → glass surface

CONTENT LAYER (solid, crisp, precise):
  ❌ Stat cards            → clean with refined shadow
  ❌ Data tables           → crisp, minimal borders
  ❌ Forms + inputs        → clean, focused
  ❌ Charts                → clear on white surface
  ❌ Page backgrounds      → subtle warm off-white
```

This is exactly how Linear, Vercel, and Apple do it. The navigation RECEDES, the content LEADS.

---

### 4. Header — The Single Most Impactful Change

Current: `border-b bg-card px-4 py-3` — solid opaque white, **not sticky**.

The biggest "wow" improvement: make it sticky + frosted. When staff scrolls the guest list, the header stays visible and shows the page content blur through it.

```css
sticky top-0 z-20
backdrop-blur-md saturate-150
bg-background/80
border-b border-border/50
shadow-[0_1px_0_rgba(0,0,0,0.04)]
```

Both partner header AND admin header get this. Same principle, same code.

---

### 5. Sidebar — Refined Without Breaking Layout

The sidebar currently sits in a `flex` layout (`w-64 shrink-0`). We **cannot** make it overlay the content without restructuring the layout (too risky). But we CAN make it feel premium:

**Partner sidebar (dark navy):**

```css
/* Current: flat bg-sidebar */
/* New: subtle gradient + refined nav items */
background: linear-gradient(180deg, #0c1627 0%, #0a1220 100%)
```

Active nav item changes from:

- Current: `bg-sidebar-primary` (solid gold background)
- New: Left gold accent bar (3px) + `bg-white/8 backdrop-blur-sm` — the "Linear style" active state

```css
/* Active item */
border-left: 3px solid #c09a2e;
background: rgba(255,255,255,0.06);
backdrop-filter: blur(8px);
```

The right edge of sidebar: replace `border-r` with `box-shadow: 1px 0 0 rgba(255,255,255,0.05)` — hairline light instead of hard divider.

---

### 6. Bento Grid Dashboard

The current dashboard has a 2×3 stat card grid. A **Bento grid** (as popularized by Apple, used on Aceternity UI, Land-book featured sites) would transform it:

```
┌──────────────┬──────────────┬──────────────────────────┐
│  Occupancy % │  Net Revenue │                          │
│   38%        │  ₹2,275      │   30-Day Revenue Chart   │
│  Large       │  Large       │   (spans 2 rows)         │
├──────────────┴──────────────┤                          │
│  Insights Panel             │                          │
│  (3 insight cards)          │                          │
└─────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────┘
```

This is more dynamic than a uniform grid, creates visual hierarchy, shows what matters most at a glance. Aceternity UI's bento grid pattern directly applies here.

---

### 7. Motion System Upgrade

Current: `150ms ease` linear transitions everywhere. Feels flat.

**Spring-based motion** (what Apple, Linear, Framer use):

```css
/* Replace all `ease` with spring cubic-bezier */
--ease-spring: cubic-bezier(0.175, 0.885, 0.32, 1.275);
--ease-smooth: cubic-bezier(0.4, 0, 0.2, 1);
--ease-out: cubic-bezier(0, 0, 0.2, 1);
```

Specific upgrades:

- **Page in**: add `translateY(6px)→0` alongside existing opacity fade
- **Nav items**: spring on hover/active
- **Button press**: `active:scale-[0.96]` + `80ms` transition — physical feedback
- **Stat cards**: `hover:scale-[1.015] hover:shadow-md` — gentle lift
- **Table rows**: Left gold accent on hover (1px) + subtle background
- **Dialog entrance**: spring overshoot (`scale(0.94)→scale(1.02)→scale(1)`)
- **Skeleton**: directional sweep gradient (already done) — ✅ keep

**Restraint rule**: No animation longer than 300ms. No animation on data content during scroll. Only chrome/UI elements animate.

---

### 8. Color System Refinement

Current background: `#f6f7f9` (cool grey)

Premium hotel management feel:

```css
--background: #f5f4f2;  /* Warm off-white, like luxury hotel paper */
--card: #ffffff;         /* Pure white surfaces stand out more against warm bg */
```

**New shadow system** (replaces flat `shadow-sm` everywhere):

```css
/* Micro shadow for floating elements */
--shadow-xs: 0 1px 2px rgba(0,0,0,0.06), 0 1px 3px rgba(0,0,0,0.04);

/* Standard card shadow */
--shadow-sm: 0 1px 3px rgba(0,0,0,0.05), 0 4px 12px rgba(0,0,0,0.04);

/* Elevated (modals, dropdowns) */
--shadow-md: 0 4px 6px rgba(0,0,0,0.04), 0 12px 40px rgba(0,0,0,0.10);
```

This replaces Tailwind's generic `shadow-sm` with precise multi-layer shadows that look like real depth (how Apple designs surfaces).

---

### 9. Border Radius — Shape Language

Current: `--radius: 0.5rem` (8px). Feels corporate.

Apple/Linear/Vercel use: 12-16px for cards, 8-12px for controls, 24px for large modals.

```css
--radius:    0.75rem;   /* 12px — default (inputs, small cards) */
--radius-md: 1rem;      /* 16px — panels, section cards */
--radius-lg: 1.25rem;   /* 20px — stat cards, main panels */
--radius-xl: 1.75rem;   /* 28px — modals, large dialogs */
```

Avatar/logo circles: CSS `clip-path: inset(0% round 28%)` creates Apple's squircle (continuous curvature, softer than a perfect circle).

---

### 10. Modal/Dialog Glass

Currently: Solid white modal on a `bg-black/50` overlay. Feels heavy.

New approach (Aceternity + Apple style):

```css
/* Modal */
background: rgba(255,255,255,0.88);
backdrop-filter: blur(20px) saturate(180%);
border: 1px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.5);
box-shadow: 0 8px 40px rgba(0,0,0,0.15), 0 2px 8px rgba(0,0,0,0.08);

/* Overlay */
background: rgba(0,0,0,0.25);
backdrop-filter: blur(3px);
```

The content behind the modal softly blurs — creates depth and focus without a harsh blackout.

---

### 11. Input / Form Field Upgrade

Currently: Basic border, gold focus ring.

Premium system:

- Unfocused: `border-border/60` (softer than current)
- Hover: `border-border` (brightens slightly)
- Focused: `border-gold-400 ring-2 ring-gold-400/20 bg-white shadow-[0_0_0_4px_rgba(192,154,46,0.08)]` — soft gold glow, like Linear's search bar
- Error: Same treatment but with `danger` color

---

### 12. What We Definitely DON'T Do

From research: These patterns are either wrong for ops SaaS or too risky:

- ❌ Aurora backgrounds / animated gradients — distracting for daily work tools
- ❌ Shader-based liquid glass — too performance-heavy, breaks accessibility
- ❌ Kinetic typography — wrong context (hotel staff is not reading marketing)
- ❌ Parallax scroll effects — operational density, not storytelling
- ❌ Switching icon libraries — massive risk, no gain
- ❌ Glass on content surfaces — Apple explicitly says don't do this
- ❌ Framer Motion library — adds 160KB bundle for effects we can do in CSS
- ❌ Dark mode by default — hotel ops staff use this on bright reception screens

---

## Master Plan — Option 4 "Floating Chrome" with Depth

### The Core Philosophy

The sidebar and header stop being **solid walls** and become **invisible scaffolding**. Only icons, text, and interactions are visible. Depth is communicated through blur, micro-borders, and edge shadows — not backgrounds.

---

### 1. `globals.css` — New Depth Utilities

**Scroll edge fade** — the nav items fade to transparent at top + bottom edges when you scroll:

```css
.scroll-fade-y {
  mask-image: linear-gradient(to bottom,
    transparent 0%, black 10%, black 90%, transparent 100%);
  -webkit-mask-image: linear-gradient(...same...);
}
```

**Page gradient** — instead of flat `#f4f3f1`, the background gets a very subtle warm radial:

```css
--background: #f4f3f1; → stays
body background → add radial-gradient(ellipse at 30% 0%, #f0ede6 0%, #f4f3f1 60%)
```

**Ghost border utility**:

```css
.ghost-border { border: 1px solid rgba(0,0,0,0.06); }
```

---

## The Color Problem with Dark Glass

The dark `rgba(8,12,22,0.65)` looks like this:

```
Heavy ■ — feels corporate, doesn't "float"
```

## 5 Translucent Color Options — Brainstorm

### 🥇 Option A — Warm Cream Frost *(Recommended)*

```css
background: rgba(255, 252, 245, 0.76);
backdrop-filter: blur(28px) saturate(180%);
border: 1px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.85);
box-shadow: inset 0 1px 0 rgba(255,255,255,0.9), 0 8px 32px rgba(0,0,0,0.08);
```

### Option C — Gold-Tinted Warm Glass

```css
backdrop-filter: blur(24px) saturate(200%);
```

### Option E — White Frost with Gold Glow

```css
border: 1px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.8);
```

- Pure white glass + very subtle gold warmth in shadow

---

### 🔴 Root cause of the differences

The admin mobile drawer had **3 compounding bugs** that made it look completely different from the partner side:

#### Bug 1 — Admin mobile was always showing as a 56px collapsed icon strip

`AdminSidebarInner` rendered `w-[56px] hover:w-[212px]` inside the drawer. On mobile touch, CSS `:hover` never fires → the sidebar was permanently 56px wide inside a 240px drawer. The result: icons on the left, empty grey space filling the rest.

**Fix:** Added `alwaysExpanded` prop — when `true`, `w-full` fills the drawer and all labels are always visible (removed all `group-hover:` constraints).

#### Bug 2 — Admin drawer had zero glass styling

The Sheet content class was just `"!w-60 gap-0 p-0 overflow-y-auto"` — plain raw white. The partner drawer had `glass-nav bg-background/95 text-foreground`.

**Fix:** Admin drawer now uses `"!w-64 glass-nav bg-background/95 text-foreground"` — exact same as partner.

#### Bug 3 — Width mismatch and partner drawer had a border

Admin drawer was 240px (`!w-60`), partner was 256px (`!w-64`). Partner also had a visible right border that violated the borderless design.

**Fix:** Both drawers now exactly `!w-64` (256px), border removed from partner.

Found it. The `scroll-fade-y` mask is `transparent 0% → rgba 5% → black 12%` — it fades the **top 12% of the nav container to invisible**, hiding Dashboard and Guest Check-in completely when the list starts from the top.

Fix: remove the top fade entirely, keep only the bottom fade:

Now fix the second mask-image block (the `-webkit-` prefixed one was already fixed, now update the unprefixed one):

Fixed and pushed. Here's what was happening:

**Root cause:** The `scroll-fade-y` CSS class used a `mask-image` that faded the **top of the nav from transparent → visible over 12% of its height**. Since Dashboard and Guest Check-in are the very first items in the list, they sat inside that 12% transparent zone and were completely invisible — appearing as a blank gap.

**Fix:** The mask now starts at fully opaque (`black 0%`) at the top so ALL items are visible from the start. The fade only applies at the **bottom** (82% → 100%) to subtly indicate there are more items below when the list overflows. This matches how every major navigation sidebar (Linear, Vercel, Notion) handles scroll indicators — you never hide the first item.

**Cursor**

I can see it clearly — the sidebar is inline (flex column), so its background creates a hard visual **partition line** between sidebar and content. This conflicts with the "no border" goal.

The fix: switch back to **absolute overlay** (sidebar floats over content, no column boundary). The collapsed state is already transparent (no panel), so no partition when collapsed. When expanded/hovered the glass floats over content — no hard wall.

Now check TypeScript and commit:

Done. Here's the thinking that led to this fix:

**Why the partition existed:**
The inline flex sidebar creates a physical DOM column — even with a transparent background, the browser creates a box. When you hover and the glass background fades in, the edge of that box becomes a hard visual wall against the content column next to it.

**Why absolute overlay eliminates it:**
The sidebar is now `position: absolute` — it sits on the same Z-layer as a floating element, not a flex sibling. There's **no column boundary**. Content renders edge-to-edge underneath. The sidebar just floats on top.

**The balance now:**

| State            | Sidebar                                                | Content                                                          |
| ---------------- | ------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Collapsed        | Transparent — zero visible panel, pure floating icons | Full width,`pl-14` (56px) protects icons from overlapping text |
| Hovered/expanded | Glass panel floats over content (Apple/iPad style)     | Content visible behind the semi-transparent glass                |

**Cursor**

Now I see it — and I found the **real root cause** of the big fonts. Let me verify my suspicion about the `cn()` class merger:

Verify and ship:

Pushed. Here's what happened, and one clarification question as you asked:

---

### 🎯 The real root cause of the big fonts — found and fixed

The "Occupied now" / "Inspection" chips (and the random oversized text elsewhere) weren't a styling mistake — they were a **class-merging bug**:

Our custom small-font classes (`text-micro` = 10px, `text-label` = 11px, `text-caption` = 12px) are defined in our own CSS, so the `tailwind-merge` library doesn't recognize them. Whenever a component combined one of them with a text **color** class — like the status chips do (`text-micro` + `text-danger`) — the library thought they were two conflicting color classes and **silently deleted the size class**. The chip then inherited the parent's 14–16px font and rendered huge.

One config fix registers these three classes properly — this repairs **every affected element platform-wide at once**: room status chips, notification text, status badges, everywhere `cn()` combined a custom size with a color.

### 🧭 Sidebar empty left area — fixed

The nav list and the spacer below it were *both* set to stretch (`flex-1`), so they split the sidebar 50/50 — nav squeezed into the top half (items cut off after "Completed Bookings"), bottom half empty navy. Now the nav takes its natural height showing **all items**, and only the leftover space sits between nav and the Upgrade Plan/footer. Also removed the fade mask that was washing out the last visible item.

### ❓ Clarification needed — notifications / "system status part"

You said the notifications and system status area are oversized and to check with you first. The font-merge fix above very likely resolves those too (they use the same affected classes). **After this deploys, can you re-check and send me a screenshot of exactly which notification/status element still looks oversized?** Then I'll fix precisely that, rather than guessing.

**Cursor**

Two runtime failures, both around the newer payment methods (Credit Card on expenses, Debit Card on checkout). Let me trace the backend validation chain for both:

Only that one migration has the bug (a later one, `d2f4b8a1c6e9`, did the same job correctly by dropping both name variants). Head is `f2e3d4c5b6a7`. Writing the repair migration:

Migration chain valid — new head. Check whether the deployment auto-runs migrations:

Render auto-runs `alembic upgrade head` on every push — so pushing this fixes production automatically:

Fixed and pushed. Both errors in your screenshots turned out to be **one single database bug** — here's the full story:

### 🎯 Root cause (one bug, two symptoms)

Your production database has been **silently rejecting Credit Card, Debit Card, Net Banking, and Other** on expenses, payments, and checkout — while Cash and UPI worked fine. That's why:

- **Expenses** → "Could not save expense — a referenced record no longer exists" (with Credit Card selected)
- **Checkout** → "An unexpected error occurred" (with Debit Card selected)

**Why:** The original database schema created the payment-method rules under prefixed names (`ck_payments_payment_method`) allowing only `cash, upi`. A later migration meant to extend the allowed methods tried to drop the rule using the **wrong (unprefixed) name** — and because it used `DROP ... IF EXISTS`, the failure was silent. It then added the new extended rule *alongside* the old one. Since the database enforces **both** rules simultaneously, the old cash/upi-only rule kept winning. The application code (schemas, models, UI) was all correct — only the live database was wrong.

### ✅ The fix

A new migration (`a1b2c3d4e5f6`) drops **every** name variant of the constraint on both tables and re-adds the single canonical rule allowing `cash, upi, card (legacy), credit_card, debit_card, bank_transfer, other`. It's idempotent and safe on any database state.

Your Render deployment runs `alembic upgrade head` on startup with auto-deploy on push — so **production repairs itself with this deploy**. Once Render finishes deploying, retry the same Credit Card expense and Debit Card checkout — both will save correctly.

### What was causing the gray boxes

Two separate causes, both now eliminated:

**1. The date/time picker triggers weren't real inputs** — they're custom buttons, and all four (`DatePicker`, `DateTimePicker`, `TimeInput`, `DateInput`) were styled `h-9` (36px) with `bg-background` (the gray page color). That's exactly the "Expense Date" and "FROM/TO" boxes the client circled. All four now use **white background, 42px height, 6px radius**.

**2. Scattered raw `<select>`/`<input>` elements had utility classes** (`bg-transparent`, `bg-background`, `rounded-lg`) that overrode my earlier base rule. The base CSS now enforces the client spec with `!important` — **white background + 42px min-height + 6px radius on every text input and select, platform-wide** — so no page-level class can ever produce a gray or short field again, including all the raw selects across expenses, payments, check-in, checkout, advance booking, team, edit-hotel, and admin pages.

One deliberate exception: the tiny month/year dropdowns *inside* the calendar popup keep their compact transparent style so the calendar header doesn't break.

Since the rule now lives at the base CSS level with highest priority, this is future-proof — any new field added later automatically complies with the spec.

It removes the classic Tesseract garbage — vowel-less letter runs like "xzkq" and "trn", stray one/two-letter fragments like "fe TT", leftover VID and phone digit runs, URLs and email fragments — while deliberately preserving real address parts: C/O and S/O markers, abbreviations like "Opp", "Nr", "St", house numbers like "12-A", "MG Road"-style initials, and bank acronyms like HDFC. I verified it against realistic junk samples, e.g. `"C/O Ramesh Kumar, fe TT H No 12-A, xzkq Nehru Street, Memnagar"` now comes out as `"C/O Ramesh Kumar, No 12-A, Nehru Street, Memnagar"`. The existing quality gate (valid pincode required, or no autofill) stays in place, so garbage still never autofills.

**1. Drafts — all of them now stay.** The root cause was storage design: drafts were saved to a single localStorage key, so each save overwrote the previous one. Now:

- Drafts live in a **list** (`dmh.checkinDrafts.v2`), newest first, capped at 10. Any draft already saved under the old single-draft key is automatically migrated into the list on first visit — nothing is lost in the upgrade.
- The check-in page shows **every saved draft** in the gold banner, each with the saved time and the guest's name (or room count) so staff can tell them apart, and each has its own **Restore** and **Discard** buttons.
- Careful edge handling: restoring a draft does **not** delete it — it's only removed after that check-in actually completes (or you discard it explicitly), so abandoning a restore mid-way never loses the draft, and completing one guest's check-in never wipes another guest's draft. Drafts are per-browser localStorage (as before), so no backend/database change is involved — the server only ever sees the final check-in.

**2. 42px heights + menu width (your screenshots).**

- The shared **filter bar** (used on Advance Bookings, Completed Bookings, Payments, Expenses) now has 42px controls across the row: the search input, the status/category selects, and the **Clear** button — all matching the date pickers, with white background and the standard radius.
- The gold **"+ New Booking"** button on Advance Bookings is 42px to line up with the search field.
- The row **actions menu** (⋮) on Advance Bookings and Current Guests is wider (`min-w-48`) with no text wrapping, so "Mark No-show" and "Cancel Booking" sit on single lines.

Expenses styling (second image).** The three circled header buttons — Add Vendor, Add Recurring Expense, Generate Due Recurring — were bare 32px outlines; they're now proper 42px buttons (white, bordered, medium weight, hover state) matching the platform form spec. The vendor dialog inputs were already on the 42px standard.

**3. Platform-wide smoothness.** The root cause of the "not smooth" feel was threefold, and I fixed each:

- Many plain elements (native selects, inline buttons, links) had **no transition at all**, so hover/focus states snapped. There's now a baseline rule in the global CSS: every link, button, input, select, and textarea transitions background, border, color, shadow, and opacity over 180ms with a smooth easing curve. It only animates paint properties (never layout), and any component with its own Tailwind transition classes still takes precedence.
- The **page fade** between routes was a 150ms flash — now a 280ms smooth-out fade (still opacity-only, which keeps the documented constraint that protects sticky footers).
- **Dropdown and select menus** popped in at 100ms — now 150ms with ease-out, so menus feel settled rather than jumpy. In-page scrolling (like validation scroll-to-section on check-in) is also smooth now.
- All of this respects `prefers-reduced-motion`: those users keep instant, animation-free behavior via the existing guard block.

Verification: TypeScript and the production build pass cleanly, and everything is pushed to `master` for the next deploy. No backend or database changes were needed for this round — it's all presentation-layer.

The plan is done and saved to `memory-bank/staffAttendancePlan.md` (committed as `b2944e4`). I studied all 10 client mockups in the `staff checkin flow` folder plus the platform's existing auth, roles, and team structures. Here's the digest:

## What this feature is

A **staff attendance module**: a Staff directory (add/edit staff with photo, department, designation, salary, shift timing, and login credentials), attendance dashboards (Today's Attendance, History, per-staff Calendar, Late & Early anomaly report), a front-desk check-in/out utility, and a **mobile self-service page** where each staff member checks themselves in with a "Face Check-In" button — all of it gated by your geofence requirement.

## The key design decisions

**Staff become real platform users.** They get logins through the existing user/membership system (temp password + forced reset already works today), with two new roles matching the mockup's access tiers: **Receptionist** (front-desk pages + attendance utility) and **General Staff** (sees only their own check-in page and history). A new `staff_profiles` table carries the HR data (staff code, department, salary, shift, photo), and `attendance_records` stores one row per staff per day with check-in/out times, GPS coordinates, distance, and the selfie.

**Geofence is enforced server-side.** The hotel gets latitude/longitude and a configurable radius (default 200 m) in Edit Hotel — with a "Use my current location" helper so the owner can set it by standing at the property. The staff phone sends its GPS position; the backend computes the distance itself and rejects check-ins outside the radius, telling the staff how far away they are. GPS accuracy gets a capped grace so a vague IP-based fix can't cheat the fence, and every record stores the coordinates and distance for audit. Two audited escape hatches exist: the front-desk utility (a manager/receptionist physically at the desk records it) and manual manager corrections with a mandatory reason. One honest caveat documented in the plan: browser GPS can be spoofed by determined users — the selfie evidence, distance audit trail, and anomaly report are the practical mitigations; true mock-location detection would need a native app.

**"Face Check-In" ships as selfie evidence first.** The check-in captures a mandatory selfie (reusing the existing guest-selfie camera pipeline) stored with the record. Actual face *matching* is flagged as an optional phase 3, since it needs a recognition service.

**Late/absent logic** derives from each staff member's shift start with a 10-minute grace, a nightly sweep marks absentees and auto-closes missing check-outs (same pattern as the existing auto-no-show sweep), and the Late & Early report surfaces anomalies.

## Delivery phasing

- **Phase 1 (MVP):** hotel geofence settings, staff directory + Add Staff, roles/permissions, self-service geofenced check-in/out with selfie, front-desk utility, Today's Attendance.
- **Phase 2:** history/calendar/late-early reports with CSV export, leave records, manager corrections UI, phone-first login.
- **Phase 3 (optional):** face matching, payroll hooks, shift rosters.

The plan file also lists six small open questions for the client (grace period, radius default, selfie mandatory or not, receptionist visibility, optional email, holiday calendar) — each with a sensible default chosen so implementation can start without waiting. Say the word and I'll begin Phase 1.

The entire staff attendance module is built, verified, and pushed — five commits ending at `59af46a`. Here's what shipped:

## Backend (database + API)

- **Migration `b7c8d9e0f1a2`**: `staff_profiles` (staff code, department, designation, employment type, shifts, weekly off, salary, photo), `attendance_records` (one per staff per day, with GPS coordinates, accuracy, computed distance, selfie key, and method stored for audit), plus four geofence columns on `hotels`. It also seeds the two new roles idempotently, so Render picks everything up on deploy without manual steps.
- **Geofence enforcement** lives in one server-side function: the **admin toggle** (off by default) and **radius (50–2000 m)** are honored; distance is computed with Haversine on the server; GPS accuracy grace is capped at 100 m so a vague fix can't cheat; violations return "You are ~X m from the property". When the toggle is off, check-in works with no location prompt at all.
- **19 API routes**: staff directory CRUD + photo, self check-in/out with selfie upload, the front-desk record endpoint (no fence — the operator's identity and the audit trail are the control), audited manual corrections requiring a note, and today/history (+CSV)/calendar/anomalies views. Owner/manager/admin get a staff profile lazily created on first self check-in, per your requirement.
- **Nightly sweep** marks absentees and auto-closes missing check-outs, joining the existing reminders loop.
- **Tests**: 18 unit tests pass locally, including the security-critical geofence cases (toggle off, capped accuracy grace, custom radius). A full integration suite (roles, corrections, tenant isolation) is in place for environments with Postgres.

## Frontend (8 pages, matching the mockups and the platform theme)

- **Staff sidebar group** with permission-gated entries. **Staff List**, **Add New Staff** (3-section form with the role radio cards and a temp-password generator; photo through the free-crop editor), and **Staff Profile** (hero, summary with salary shown as "Confidential" without permission, This Month stats, recent attendance — and the **self check-in card on your own profile** for managers/admins).
- **My Attendance** — the mobile-first self-service page: live clock, status pill, the big round **Face Check-In** button (GPS fix → selfie → check-in), clear danger callouts for denied location or geofence violations, today's summary, and a month mini-calendar. Everything is done and pushed — Vercel (frontend) and Render (backend, which runs the migration automatically) will pick up the latest `master` on their next deploy. Once the deploy finishes, here's how you use it:

## One-time setup (as Owner)

1. **Set the property location** — go to **Edit Hotel**, find the new "Restrict staff check-in to hotel premises" block. Stand at the hotel (or use the property's coordinates), tap **"Use my current location"** to fill latitude/longitude, set the **radius** (default 200 m, allowed 50–2000 m), tick the **toggle**, and Save. If you leave the toggle off, attendance still works — just without the location restriction.
2. **Add your staff** — open **Staff → Staff List → + Add Staff**. Fill the three sections: personal info (photo optional), employment info (department, designation, shift start/end — the shift start is what "Late" is measured against, with a 10-minute grace), and login credentials. Pick the access tier:

   - **Hotel Manager** — full access including the staff module
   - **Receptionist / Front Desk** — guest check-in/bookings pages + can record other staff's attendance at the desk
   - **General Staff** — sees only their own check-in page

   Give them the temporary password (there's a Generate button). Email is optional — phone is enough.

## Daily use

- **Staff member (own phone):** they log in at digitalmyhotels.vercel.app with their phone-based account and temp password, are forced to set a new password, and land directly on **My Attendance**. They tap the big **Face Check-In** button → the browser asks for location → they take a selfie → checked in. At shift end, **Check Out**. If they're outside the radius, they get "You are ~450 m from the property" and can't check in.
- **You and managers/admins:** the same check-in card appears on your **own Staff Profile** (and My Attendance works for you too).
- **Front desk:** **Staff → Staff Check-in/out** — search a staff member by name/ID/mobile and tap Check In / Check Out on their card. No location needed there since the operator is at the desk; every such record stores who performed it.

## Monitoring

- **Today's Attendance** — live stat cards (Total/Present/Working/Checked Out/Absent/Late), filterable table with a day pager and CSV export.
- **Attendance History** — date-range records with working-hours bars and export.
- **Calendar** — any staff member's month at a glance.
- **Late & Early Reports** — late arrivals, early check-outs, and missing check-outs. Anyone who forgets to check out gets auto-closed at shift end overnight and flagged here; absentees are marked automatically too. Managers can correct any record, but a reason is mandatory and it's audited.

## What's optionally next (not required to use the feature)

These are the phase 2/3 items from the plan, whenever you want them: a **leave management** flow (apply/approve), **phone-number login** (currently phone-created accounts log in via their internal email — I can surface that cleanly or add true phone login), real **face matching** on the selfie (currently the selfie is stored as evidence), and **payroll hooks** (salary × attendance). Just say which one and I'll pick it up.

One practical tip for the first live test: browser geolocation requires HTTPS — the Vercel domain is fine — and the staff member must allow the location prompt. If GPS accuracy indoors is poor, the fence allows up to 100 m of GPS-accuracy grace on top of your radius, so a 200 m radius behaves sensibly inside the building.

The deep-verification round is complete and pushed (commit `2470cc6`). Your screenshot's bug plus four more issues found and fixed:

**1. Sidebar double-highlight (your screenshot).** Root cause: active detection used simple prefix matching, so on `/staff/checkin` both "Staff List" (`/staff` is a prefix) and "Staff Check-in/out" lit up gold. It's now **longest-prefix-wins** — exactly one item, the most specific match, is ever active. This also pre-empted the same bug on Attendance History/Reports (which share the `/staff/attendance` prefix with Today's Attendance), and the same fix went into the mobile tab bar.

**2. Double check-in on Face Check-In (would have been the next bug report).** The shared camera component calls `onCapture` and then *also* `onClose` — my check-in card treated `onClose` as "skipped, proceed without selfie", so every selfie capture fired the check-in **twice**: success, then an ugly "Already checked in" error a second later. Now a guard ensures the check-in posts exactly once per camera session; cancel/camera-unavailable still proceeds once without the selfie so no device is locked out.

**3. Dates were UTC, not local — wrong for India.** Seven places across the staff pages used `toISOString()`, which converts to UTC first. For IST users before 5:30 AM, "today" was yesterday; the Today's Attendance day pager was *always* off by a day when stepping; month calendars opened on the wrong month at month boundaries. Added a `localYmd()` helper and fixed the day pager, history/report default ranges, both month calendars, the joining-date default, and the DOB max.

**4. On-leave staff misreported.** A staff member marked "On Leave" with no attendance record showed a red "NOT CHECKED IN" chip in the Staff List — now shows the blue "ON LEAVE" chip.

**5. Housekeeping** — removed the stale test-comparison worktree and extracted-zip folders from earlier sessions.

This calls for a real logic audit, not just polish. I re-reviewed the whole flow and found genuine illogicalities plus missing insight surfaces. Here's the audit, then I'll implement the fixes:

**Logic flaws found:**

1. **"Absent" is wrong all morning** — Today's stats count everyone who hasn't arrived yet as absent, so at 9 AM the whole hotel looks absent. Should only count after their shift start + grace has passed.
2. **Auto-closed checkouts destroy their own evidence** — the sweep sets `method_out="manual"`, so the Missing Check-outs report can never find them afterward. Needs a distinct `auto` method that stays visible in anomalies.
3. **Check-out is geofence-blocked** — a staffer who leaves and forgets can *never* check out (fence blocks them from home), forcing bad auto-closed data. Logical fix: check-**in** stays strictly fenced; check-**out** is allowed anywhere but flagged with the recorded distance when off-site.
4. **Overnight shifts break the math** — a shift like 22:00–07:00 computes "early out" against same-day 07:00. Needs next-day handling.
5. **Grace period is hardcoded** (10 min) — should be admin-configurable next to the geofence settings.
6. **The evidence is invisible** — we store GPS distance, accuracy, method, selfie, and who recorded it, but **no manager UI shows any of it**. That's the biggest missing insight: a per-record detail view with the selfie and distance.
7. **Calendar**: today isn't highlighted, future days render blank instead of "scheduled".
8. **No aggregate insight line** — on-time rate and average working hours are trivially computable but shown nowhere.

The full insight round is shipped (commit `a085b6b`). I audited the entire flow database → backend → frontend, found five genuine logic flaws, fixed them all, and added the evidence surfaces that were missing. Summary:

## Logic flaws fixed

1. **"Absent" was wrong all morning.** The Today's Attendance dashboard counted everyone who hadn't arrived yet as absent — at 9 AM the whole hotel looked absent. Now a no-show only counts as absent once their **shift start + grace has passed** (in the hotel's timezone); staff without a configured shift are never marked absent mid-day, and past days count correctly.
2. **Auto-closed checkouts erased their own evidence.** The nightly sweep labeled them "manual", so the Missing Check-outs report could never find them again. They now get a distinct **"Auto-closed"** method that stays permanently visible in the anomalies report, the sweep waits 4 hours past shift end before closing (no premature closes), and it handles overnight shifts.
3. **Check-out was geofence-blocked — a data trap.** A staffer who left the site and forgot could never check out, forcing inflated auto-closed hours. New policy: check-**in** stays strictly fenced, check-**out** is always allowed but the distance is measured and **off-site checkouts are flagged** on the record ("off-site check-out ~830 m from property") — visible to managers.
4. **Overnight shifts (22:00–07:00) broke early-out math** — now computed against the correct next-day shift end.
5. **The 10-minute late grace was hardcoded** — now an admin setting ("Late grace minutes", 0–120) right in the Edit Hotel geofence section, with a migration applied and verified.

## New insight surfaces

- **Attendance Details dialog** — the biggest gap: we stored the selfie, GPS distance, accuracy, method, and operator on every record but showed none of it. Now every row on Today's Attendance has **View Details**: the check-in selfie photo, check-in/check-out distances with ±accuracy, method chips (Self GPS / Front Desk / Manual / Auto-closed), late/early minutes, who recorded it, and any off-site or auto-close notes. This is what makes the geofence + Face Check-In actually *verifiable* by a manager.
- **Daily insight line** under the table: on-time rate percentage and average working hours for the day.
- **Calendar**: today gets the gold focus ring, and future days render as dashed "Scheduled" cells instead of blank.

**Verified:** all 76 staff/unit tests green against real Postgres, the new migration applied cleanly, production build green, English + Hindi complete. The remaining known-future items (split shifts, half-day leave, attendance notifications, payroll hooks, face matching, shift rosters) are documented in the memory bank so nothing gets silently forgotten.

**Cursor**

Two things to run down: the "Something went wrong" on Save Staff, and the clock/time-picker consistency. Let me look at how the API client classifies errors and what the time components do:

Now the Save Staff failure. Checking how `parseError` behaves on non-JSON responses, then reproducing the exact screenshot payload in a test:

Both issues fixed and pushed (commit `6dcb14a`).

**1. The "Something went wrong" on Save Staff — root cause found.** It wasn't the create failing: the staff member was actually being **created successfully**. The bug was in the photo upload step right after — the photo endpoint returns *204 No Content*, and the shared `apiUpload` helper unconditionally tried to parse the empty response as JSON, which threw a raw `SyntaxError` that the form displayed as the generic error. So you saw a failure banner for a save that had already worked (and a retry would then correctly complain the phone number was taken — the row already existed). Fixes:

- `apiUpload` now handles 204 responses (same guard `apiFetch` already had), which also protects any future upload endpoint.
- Defense in depth: even if a photo upload genuinely fails, the form no longer reports it as a create failure — you get "Staff saved, but the photo upload failed — retry from Edit Staff" as a warning, and you still land on the new profile.

One heads-up: if you hit this on the live site, the staff member from that attempt probably **exists already** — check the Staff List before re-creating them, or you'll get a duplicate-phone error.

**2. The custom clock, everywhere.** You're right that there were two different clocks: the `DateTimePicker` (check-out date/time etc.) uses the platform's custom panel with gold scrollable 24-hour hour/minute columns, but the standalone `TimeInput` (Shift Start/End on Add Staff, check-in/check-out time settings, and every other pure-time field) was opening the **OS-native** time picker. I rewrote `TimeInput` to open the exact same custom clock panel — same gold selection, same scrollable 00–23 hour and 5-minute-step columns, same "Time (24h)" header and Done footer, positioned so parent containers can't clip it, closing on outside click/scroll/Escape. Since every time field in the system goes through this one component, the whole platform now shares a single clock UI.

Note the custom clock uses 5-minute steps (the platform standard from `DateTimePicker`), so shift times like the "08:17" in your screenshot will now snap to 08:15/08:20 — consistent with everywhere else.

Verification: TypeScript and the production build pass; pushed to `master` for deploy.

## What was wrong and how to fix it right now

**The root cause:** The backend on Render is running with `STORAGE_BACKEND=local`, meaning files are written to Render's container disk. Render's free-tier containers have an **ephemeral filesystem** — it gets completely wiped on every deploy, restart, or sleep. So every time you deploy a new commit (which we've been doing all session), all uploaded files disappear. The staff row exists in Postgres (which persists), but the photo and selfie object keys stored there point to files that no longer exist on disk.

**The fix:** Set up Backblaze B2 (free up to 10 GB storage + 1 GB/day downloads — more than enough) and add these environment variables to your **Render dashboard** → your backend service → **Environment**:

```
STORAGE_BACKEND=b2
B2_ENDPOINT=https://s3.us-west-004.backblazeb2.com
B2_KEY_ID=<your Application Key ID>
B2_APPLICATION_KEY=<your Application Key>
B2_BUCKET_NAME=digitalmyhotels
B2_PUBLIC_BASE_URL=https://digitalmyhotels.s3.us-west-004.backblazeb2.com
B2_REGION=us-west-004
```

**Step by step to get those values:**

1. Go to [backblaze.com](https://www.backblaze.com) → sign up free
2. **Buckets** → Create a Bucket named `digitalmyhotels` → note the region (e.g. `us-west-004`)
3. **App Keys** → Add a New Application Key → name it `digitalmyhotels-render` → allow read/write on `digitalmyhotels` bucket → Create Key → **copy both the Key ID and the Application Key right now** (you can't see the key again)
4. Set `B2_ENDPOINT` to `https://s3.<your-region>.backblazeb2.com`
5. Set `B2_PUBLIC_BASE_URL` to `https://digitalmyhotels.s3.<your-region>.backblazeb2.com`
6. Add all 7 env vars to Render → Save → the service will redeploy

**After the deploy, verify it works:**

- Open **Edit Hotel** in the app
- Scroll to the bottom → click **"Test Storage"**
- You should see `✓ Storage OK — B2 (digitalmyhotels)` in green
- If you see an error, the Key ID, Application Key or endpoint URL has a typo

**What's been shipped in this commit:**

- The backend now **logs a warning at startup** if `STORAGE_BACKEND=local` outside development — this will appear in Render's logs immediately after your next deploy so the issue is unmissable
- `GET /api/v1/storage/health` does a real write → read → delete test and reports persistence
- The **"Test Storage"** card in Edit Hotel shows the exact env vars to paste if local is detected
- `backend/.env.example` is a complete reference with step-by-step Backblaze B2 instructions

Once B2 is configured, staff photos uploaded via Add Staff and selfies captured during Face Check-In will persist permanently across all future deploys.

All 10 bugs fixed and pushed (commit `38bad56`). Here's what changed and why:

---

**1. X button causing accidental check-in (your reported bug) — FIXED.**
The `FaceCapture` component now has three distinct callbacks:

- **`onCapture(file)`** — selfie taken → proceed with check-in ✅
- **`onCancel()`** — X button pressed → **abort entirely**, reset state, nothing posted, no check-in ✅
- **`onError()`** — camera unavailable OR "Skip" tapped → proceed without selfie evidence ✅

Previously `onSkip` handled both cancel and camera-failure — a single code path that always posted a check-in. Now the X button is a genuine cancel.

**2. Can't re-check-in after checkout — FIXED.**
Staff can now check back in the same day after a completed checkout. Common scenario: staff goes home for lunch and returns, or finishes an early shift and helps out again. The system resets the checkout fields, preserves the previous checkout time in the audit note ("re-check-in, prev out: 14:30"), and starts a fresh check-in. Trying to check in while still currently checked-in (no checkout yet) is still blocked with a clear message: "You are already checked in. Please check out first."

**3. Checkout GPS blocked checkout — FIXED.**
If the fence is on but GPS is unavailable at checkout time (staff already left the property, weak signal indoors), checkout no longer throws a 422. It goes through, flags "off-site" in the note, and the manager sees it in the anomalies report. Blocking checkout was producing worse data than allowing it.

**4. 5 notification events now wired:**

- **Late arrival** → notifies manager immediately when `late_minutes > 0`
- **Absent** → notifies manager when nightly sweep marks a no-show
- **Missing checkout** → notifies manager when auto-close fires
- **Leave requested** → notifies owner/manager when staff apply
- **Leave decided** → notifies the staff member when approved/rejected

All appear in the `/notifications` panel with direct links to the relevant attendance page.

**5. Working-while-late status** — the check-in card now shows a yellow "Working (Late)" pill correctly, and "Check In Again" button when the day's session is complete.

Push succeeded (`38bad56..67d9d3e`). Two stray screenshots got swept into the commit — removing them:

The `*.png` ignore is too broad (would silently ignore future frontend assets) — scoping it to root only:

All 12 bugs are fixed, verified, and pushed (commits `67d9d3e` → `0a9f61c`). TypeScript check clean, production build green, ruff clean, and 18 invoice/auth/password tests pass — the one failing test (`test_password_request_hierarchy`) is one of the 3 pre-existing failures we documented before this batch, unrelated to today's changes. Here is every issue and exactly how it was solved:

**1. Small loader appearing during login/logout**
The bottom-right route loader fired on *every* pathname change, including auth screens where the full-screen master loader was already showing. In `route-loader.tsx` I added an exclusion list (`/`, `/login`, `/forgot-password`, `/change-password`, `/suspended`) and also suppress the loader when *coming from* one of those routes — so the login → dashboard boot transition shows only the master loader. It now appears exclusively for navigation inside the system.

**2. Plans page — capital first letter**
The plan code under each plan name ("plan-1m") now renders with Tailwind's `capitalize`, so it displays as "Plan-1m".

**3. Suspended screen**
Widened the message to `max-w-xl` (reads in ~3 lines), added a contact card with **Email Id: support@digitalmyhotels.com** and **Phone: +91 99986 22002** (both tappable — mailto/tel links), and the Sign out button now explicitly lands on the login screen after logging out. Button is 42px.

**4. Reset-password screen height**
The "Request reset" button is now `h-[42px]`, matching the 42px input beside it.

**5. Forgot password — phone for super admin**
The password-request rows now carry the requester's **phone number** from the backend (`_request_row` includes `user.phone`), and both the Super Admin Password Requests page and the hotel-admin Team banner display it prominently in gold as a tap-to-call link — since the new password is communicated over the phone. If no phone is on file, super admin sees a red "No phone number on file" warning. Internal synthetic emails (`@noemail.example`) are hidden instead of shown.

**6. Dashboard shuffle**
Final order now matches the client's arrow: room-status chips → Arrivals/Collection/Quick Actions → Guest Mix/Weekly → Revenue by Room Type → **Smart Insights + KPIs + 30-day chart** → In-House Guests table.

**7. Invoice**
The guest address was already removed in the current code — the client's screenshot predates that deploy, so this redeploy resolves it. Line items are now capitalized ("Other: Dinner Included", "Restaurant: …") in three layers: at invoice generation (backend), in the styled card, and in the PDF — the latter two also fix invoices generated before this change.

**8. GST functionality (three modes)**
The math was already correct (`no_gst` → zero tax; `included_by_hotel` → tax-inclusive pricing so nothing is added on the customer side; `included_by_customer` → GST added on top and displayed). What was missing was the *hiding* for No-GST hotels, now implemented everywhere: the **GST & Tax sidebar item disappears** (sidebar checks the hotel's GST registration), the **checkout settlement GST row is hidden**, and the **invoice card and PDF omit the GST line**.

**9. Add New Hotel cut off**
The scroll area now has `pb-32` so the fixed Save Draft / Add Hotel bar never covers the last section, and both buttons are 42px.

**10. All Customers — Export CSV**
Added an Export CSV button (42px) that downloads the full filtered list (name, masked contact, hotel, city) — same privacy masking as the on-screen table.

**11. Super admin Settings**
Added an **Edit** button on the Profile panel opening a dialog to edit name and phone — backed by a new audited `PATCH /api/v1/auth/me` endpoint with phone normalization and uniqueness checks (email stays read-only since it's the login identity). "Change password" is now "Change **P**assword" everywhere (heading, button, page title).

**12. Change-password Go Back**
Added a proper Go Back button that returns super admins to `/admin/settings` and partners to `/settings`. It's deliberately hidden during a *forced* password reset so that flow can't be escaped. Buttons standardized to 42px.

All changes cover both mobile and web (the loader exclusion applies to the mobile-centered variant too, suspended/settings layouts are responsive), and Hindi translations were added for every new string. One note: the invoice address and all these fixes will be visible once Vercel/Render pick up this push.

**What actually happened:** this wasn't a bug in the codebase. The version of `staff/attendance/page.tsx` on disk and in git was perfectly healthy (it built cleanly an hour ago). The corruption existed only in your **open editor tab as an unsaved change**: 10 lines in the middle of the import block got accidentally deleted — most likely a stray keystroke or drag while the file was open. That single deletion removed:

- the end of the `lucide-react` icon import (`UserX,` and `} from "lucide-react"`), which caused all the icons to appear as if they were being imported from `dropdown-menu`
- the `toast` (sonner) import
- the `useMutation` / `useQueryClient` (react-query) import
- the `PartnerHeader` import
- the `DropdownMenu` component imports

That one missing chunk explains every single one of the 33 red errors you pasted — they were all downstream symptoms of the same deletion.

**The fix:** I restored the missing block exactly as it exists in git, then verified the file byte-for-byte matches the committed version. While there, I also cleaned up a pre-existing style warning by merging the two separate `@tanstack/react-query` import lines into one. `tsc --noEmit` passes with zero errors, and the cleanup is pushed as `16971d0`.

The two `_reorder2.js` warnings in your list were from a temporary helper script I used earlier for the dashboard reorder — it was already deleted, so those diagnostics were stale and will disappear on their own.

All green. Updating the memory bank and shipping:

GST is now implemented properly across all three modes and pushed (`2c956f0`). Here's the in-depth story:

## Why the client was right — the root cause

When a hotel was created with "GST Included by Hotel", the system stored a `tax_inclusive_pricing` flag… **and then never read it anywhere**. Every calculation in the product treated the room rate as tax-exclusive and added GST on top. So mode 1 and mode 2 behaved identically, and the earlier fix only handled hiding for No-GST hotels. Two bonus bugs surfaced during the audit: the check-in balance screen invented a **5% fallback GST even for No-GST hotels**, and restaurant billing nagged No-GST hotels to "configure GST".

## How the three modes work now

The mode is encoded in two existing flags (no migration needed) and exposed as one concept, `gst_mode`, through the API:

**1. GST Included by Hotel** — the price the hotel enters is exactly what the customer pays. The GST engine (`calculate_gst(..., inclusive=True)`) now *extracts* the tax from within that price — taxable base + tax always reconstruct the entered amount to the rupee. The customer never sees a GST line on the checkout screen, invoice card, or PDF (the subtotal is shown gross so the numbers add up), but the extracted tax is still recorded on every charge and invoice, so the hotel's own **GST & Tax page and reports show their liability** — exactly "added in hotel side, no customer side."

**2. GST Including Customer** — unchanged behavior: GST added on top of the amount and displayed everywhere (checkout GST row, invoice GST row, check-in breakdown, PDF).

**3. No GST Applicable** — zero tax, and the GST flow disappears from the partner system entirely: the **GST & Tax sidebar item**, the checkout/invoice/PDF GST rows, the check-in GST tile (and its phantom 5%), and in **restaurant billing** the GST KPI card, the three GST table columns, and the "configure GST" warning.

This was applied at every calculation site — additional charges, the current-guests due column, the checkout quote/commit calculator, final settlement, and invoice generation — so the numbers agree by construction across all screens.

## Changing the mode after creation

Super admin still sets the mode at Add Hotel (that mapping already worked). New: the partner **Settings → GST tab now has the three-mode selector** (replacing the bare "GST Registered" checkbox), with plain-language hints for each mode, in English and Hindi. Changes are audited, including the pricing-flag flip.

## Verification

- 4 new unit tests lock in the inclusive math (exact gross reconstruction, interstate, unregistered) — 11 GST unit tests pass.
- All 31 integration tests covering GST, invoices, checkout, charges, and the full stay lifecycle pass against the local Postgres — confirming existing "Including Customer" hotels see zero change in their numbers.
- `ruff`, `tsc`, and the production build are all clean.

One deployment note: existing hotels created as "Included by Hotel" already have the right flags stored, so this fix activates the correct behavior for them automatically — but invoices generated *before* today keep their old stored amounts (invoices are immutable records; only their display now hides the GST row).

All 10 issues are solved and pushed as `a040a3b`. One important note first: I found substantial uncommitted work already in the working tree covering part of this batch (from a parallel session) — I reviewed every line of it for correctness, fixed what was wrong, filled the two missing issues, and committed it all together. Here is each issue with the exact approach and justification:

**1. Add New Hotel — bottom cut off.** The earlier `pb-32` was bumped to `pb-44` (176px of clearance), and two real causes were fixed: the fixed action bar now stops at the sidebar edge on desktop (`lg:left-64`, it previously stretched under the sidebar) and gets safe-area padding so mobile browser chrome can't clip the buttons. Proof: the bar is ~66px tall; clearance is now 2.6× that.

**2. GST functionality.** Shipped earlier today (commit `2c956f0`) — the root cause was that `tax_inclusive_pricing` was stored but never read, so "Included by Hotel" behaved like "Including Customer". Now: inclusive mode extracts tax from inside the price (customer total never changes, hotel-side GST recorded), customer mode adds and displays it, and No-GST hides the entire GST flow — sidebar item, checkout row, invoice rows, PDF row, check-in tile, restaurant-billing columns. Locked in by 4 new unit tests. If the client tested before this deploy, that explains the re-report.

**3. First letter uppercase.** The plan **code** fix was already committed; this batch extends it to the plan **name** on plan cards and the plan-name column in all five admin tables (hotels, expired, registrations, dashboard renewals, dashboard expired). All use CSS `capitalize`, so "plan-1m" renders "Plan-1m" without touching stored data.

**4. "Clicking then go plan page."** Found the exact cause: on the admin dashboard's Quick Actions, both **Settings** and **Generate Report** were hardcoded to `/admin/plans` — clicking them dumped the client on the Plans page. Settings now goes to `/admin/settings` and Generate Report to the new `/admin/revenue`.

**5. Recently Expired / Expired Hotels.** Two backend bugs: (a) hotels manually marked expired with no subscription row were silently dropped by a NULL-unsafe date filter — now included; (b) there was no "about to expire" concept at all. The hotels endpoint now accepts `expiring_within=5`, and the Recently Expired view shows hotels expiring within 5 days with an amber "Expires in Nd" badge alongside recently-expired ones (red "Expired") — exactly the client's "manage hotels about to expire" ask.

**6. Total Revenue screen.** New `GET /super-admin/revenue` endpoint (platform grand total + per-hotel breakdown of completed payments, searchable, paginated) with a new `/admin/revenue` page, a sidebar entry, and the dashboard's Total Revenue card now links to it. The number is computed with the identical query the dashboard card uses, so they always agree.

**7. Super admin reset password.** The hotel detail now returns `owner_user_id`, and the hotel Edit page has a "Reset Owner Password" block that calls the same audited endpoint the Password Requests screen uses — sessions revoked, change forced at next login. The super admin no longer has to wait for a request to come in.

**8. Expired hotel showing "Active".** Root cause: `Hotel.status` is never flipped automatically when a plan lapses — the lists derive expiry from the subscription but the detail endpoint returned the raw column. The detail now computes an *effective status*: if the latest subscription's expiry + grace is past, it reports "expired", matching the lists.

**9. Custom plan extension.** New `POST /super-admin/hotels/{id}/subscription/extend` with `days` (1–365): extends from `max(today, current expiry)` so a 5-day grant always gives 5 usable days even on a lapsed plan, refreshes subscription status, reactivates the hotel so it leaves the expired lists, and is audited. Suspended hotels are refused (unsuspending is a deliberate separate action). Gold "Extend" button with a days-input dialog on both the Expired page and the hotels list, English + Hindi.

**10. Edit hotel "doesn't update".** The save itself was verified correct (every call is bound to the target hotel's `X-Hotel-Id`, and the backend PATCH persists all fields). The real cause was **stale cache**: after saving, the hotel *list* queries kept showing pre-edit values for up to 30 seconds, which reads as "didn't save". All admin list caches are now invalidated on save. Bonus fix found during review: the customers CSV export was silently failing because the frontend requested `limit=5000` against a backend cap of 100 (a 422) — cap raised and errors now surface as toasts.

**Verification:** ruff clean, `tsc` clean, production build green, and 14/15 super-admin/subscription integration tests pass — the single failure is the documented pre-existing `test_super_admin_control` conftest state-pollution issue, unrelated to these changes (confirmed by its failure signature). Everything is live once Vercel and Render pick up the push.

**Cursor**

This is a ~35-issue batch with one truly critical architectural concern (cross-hotel draft leakage), and you've asked for a deep investigation and plan with **strictly no implementation**. Let me ground the most load-bearing issues in the actual code first, then present the full analysis and plan.

Investigation done — I've grounded every major claim in the actual code and have **not changed anything**. Here is the full analysis, what I found as root causes, my proposed approach for each, and the risks each change carries.

---

## A. CRITICAL — Multi-hotel isolation (the draft leak)

**What I found (confirmed in code):** Check-in drafts are stored in the browser's `localStorage` under one global key — `dmh.checkinDrafts.v2` — with **no hotel ID anywhere in the key or the draft data**. localStorage belongs to the browser, not to the logged-in account. So: same laptop, log out of Hotel A, log into Hotel B → Hotel B sees Hotel A's drafts. This exactly matches the client's complaint.

**Important nuance:** the backend is *not* leaking. Every backend query I audited runs through `tenant.require_hotel()` scoping. The leak class is client-side browser storage. The same audit found the super-admin Add Hotel draft (`dmh.addHotelDraft`) is also global, but that's super-admin-only, so it's low risk.

**Plan:**

1. Namespace every hotel-scoped localStorage key by hotel: `dmh.checkinDrafts.v2:<hotelId>`. Draft read/write helpers take `activeHotelId`; drafts saved under one hotel are invisible to every other.
2. Migration decision needed (question 3 below): drafts already saved on devices have no hotel tag — the only safe option is to adopt them into the hotel that is active when the code first runs on that device.
3. Systematic audit pass: every `localStorage`/`sessionStorage` usage and every React Query key gets checked for hotel scoping (most query keys already include `activeHotelId`; I'll verify all of them, plus clear the query cache on hotel switch).
4. A matching backend audit pass over all list endpoints as a defence-in-depth verification (I expect it to pass — it did on spot checks).

**Risk:** the migration step. If a receptionist saved a Hotel A draft and the first login after deploy is Hotel B, that draft would adopt into B. It's a one-time, low-probability event, but it's the honest trade-off vs deleting all existing drafts.

## B. Expired hotels can still log in and operate (Raj Place)

**What I found:** expiry enforcement is **cosmetic only**. The frontend shows a dismissible modal + banner; the backend blocks only *transactions*, and only when the plan's `block_transactions_after_expiry` flag is on. Login, dashboard, reports, check-ins — all work forever after expiry.

**Plan (two layers, server-side first):**

1. **Backend (authoritative):** extend the tenant dependency so a hotel whose subscription is past expiry + grace is rejected with `subscription_expired` on **all** partner endpoints except a small whitelist: auth, subscription status, plan catalogue, renewal request. This is the same pattern the suspension flow already uses, so it inherits well-tested machinery.
2. **Frontend:** a full-screen non-dismissible "Plan Expired" overlay (like the suspension screen) whose only actions are *View Plans / Request Renewal* and *Logout*.
3. The "Pending needed in expired modal popup" item fits here: once a renewal request is submitted, the expired overlay should show its **Pending** status so the hotel knows the request is with the super admin.

**Decision I need from you (question 1):** the client wrote "plan expired, don't login" — but if login is blocked completely, the owner can't see plans or request renewal, and every renewal becomes a phone call. I recommend: allow login, but lock the entire UI to the plan/renewal screen. This also interacts with the new **Extend** feature — an extension immediately reopens access, which is the point of the grant.

## C. Documents cluster — Aadhaar photos not showing, draft/autofill losing images

Four reported symptoms, three distinct causes:

1. **Uploaded Aadhaar/photos don't show (screenshot 2).** Prime suspect is not code: production Render still runs `STORAGE_BACKEND=local` on an ephemeral disk unless the B2 environment variables were set (we shipped the diagnostics for exactly this — Edit Hotel → Test Storage). Files upload fine, then vanish on dyno restart. **Action: run the storage health check on production; if it says "local", the fix is setting the B2 env vars in Render, not code.** If storage is healthy, then the bug is display wiring and moves to the check-in section rework below.
2. **Draft restore loses co-guest data and documents.** Structural: uploaded files are held in React state as `File` objects and can't survive localStorage. The draft schema stores text fields only. Plan: extend the draft schema to store co-guest text data fully (currently partial), and for images either (a) persist uploads to the server immediately on selection (tied to hotel + draft id, garbage-collected), or (b) store compressed base64 in the draft (localStorage ~5MB cap makes this fragile with multiple guests). I recommend (a); it also fixes "restore doesn't show rooms amount" since rates get re-fetched.
3. **Autofill doesn't bring documents / autofill button missing.** The search+autofill endpoint returns identity fields; documents are separate. Plan: include document references in the autofill payload and render previews; and fix the conditional that hides the Autofill button (it renders only in one branch of the identity section state machine — same rework as section D).
4. **ID photo viewer readability:** open the viewer at ~2× initial zoom with the existing pinch/drag, per the screenshot.

## D. Check-in flow state machine + room logic

**Guest identity bugs (critical screenshot 11):** "Create Guest → blank", "Confirm hides customer", "no Update button after edits". The identity section is a hand-rolled state machine (search → selected → confirmed) and its states drop the form contents instead of keeping them visible read-only. Plan: rework to explicit states — *empty → editing → confirmed (read-only summary card with Change + Edit) → editing (dirty, shows Update)* — so confirming never blanks anything and edits always surface an Update action. Same fix covers primary and co-guests (and the double `++` icon duplication and "Add Guest" polish marked in the same screenshot).

**Room selection (screenshot 5 + "check the logic in depth"):** The availability API classifies by **date overlap** — a room physically occupied *today* whose booking ends today is offered as "available" (with a small "Occupied now" hint) even for a check-in starting *now*. That's correct for future bookings, wrong for same-day walk-ins. Plan: for bookings starting today, rooms whose current stay hasn't checked out yet (and rooms in cleaning-in-progress) move to the "coming free" section — not selectable — until checkout/cleaning completes; future-dated bookings keep today's behavior. Reserved/maintenance rules stay as-is (already excluded by overlap). Advance-booking overlap uses the same server logic, so it inherits the fix.

**Housekeeping status not updating without refresh:** classic stale React Query cache — the housekeeping mutation invalidates its own page's queries but not the check-in picker's `room-availability` / dashboard's room-status keys. Plan: shared invalidation (invalidate the room-status key family on every room-status mutation) plus `refetchOnWindowFocus` + a modest `refetchInterval` (~30s) on room-status queries, so multi-device desks converge without manual refresh. The client's "audit all flows for similar sync issues" = a sweep of all mutations for missing cross-page invalidations.

**Room-status count (screenshot 14):** the CLEANING card counts `cleaning_required + cleaning_in_progress + inspection_required` while the "Cleaning Required" filter chip lists a subset — counts look wrong but are "about to clean" vs "cleaning". Plan: make the card count match the chips (or split the label into "To clean"), presented consistently.

## E. Money cluster

1. **Card + Others KPI cards:** Payment Details already has them in code (the screenshot shows them live). Plan: add the same two to **Hotel Expenses** KPIs and the **Dashboard Today's Collection** card (backend summaries need card/others buckets where missing).
2. **Invoice "not generated" (screenshot 16):** confirmed root cause — invoices are created **lazily**, only when someone clicks Print/Download/Email after checkout. Complete a checkout without touching those buttons → no invoice ever exists. Plan: auto-generate the invoice inside the atomic checkout commit (backend), so every completed booking has one; the buttons then just fetch it. Risk considered: double generation is already guarded by the existing "active invoice exists" conflict check.
3. **"Invoice ID wrong":** numbering is a per-hotel sequence assigned at *generation time* — because generation is lazy, INV numbers don't follow booking order. Auto-generation at checkout (above) makes numbering chronological going forward. No renumbering of history (numbers are legal artifacts).
4. **"GST not showing" / "missing GST in print and modal":** needs a reproduction against the hotel's `gst_mode` — after the three-mode implementation, hiding GST is *correct* for No-GST and Included-by-Hotel hotels. Plan: verify the reporting hotel's mode first; if it's an Including-Customer hotel, then trace the print/modal paths. I will not "fix" this blind — it risks undoing the mode logic the client asked for.
5. **Edit Stay due ₹784 vs 904 (screenshot 13):** suspicious — the Edit Stay dialog computes due from booking totals which historically excluded GST (there's a known-fixed analogue in Current Guests). Plan: make Edit Stay use the same GST-aware settlement the checkout uses.
6. **Additional charges detail (screenshot 15):** show the restaurant/damage/other breakdown consistently in payment details/completed-booking panels, not just checkout.
7. **"Add below Payment Details BK-0023 / Invoices BK-0023":** display the booking number under those two screens' rows/preview (it's already a column in payments; the invoice preview lacks it — add booking number to the invoice card + list).
8. **"Powered by DigitalMyHotels" on invoices + per-hotel super-admin toggle:** new `show_powered_by` flag on hotel settings (default on), rendered on the invoice card + PDF footer; toggle in the super-admin hotel edit page.

## F. Platform features

1. **Team limit (default 5, super-admin adjustable):** confirmed no cap exists in `team.py`. Plan: `hotels.max_team_members` column (default 5, migration), enforced at member creation (counting **active, non-owner** members — question 4), a limit field in super-admin Add Hotel + hotel edit, and a clear "limit reached — contact support" message in the partner Team page. Hotels already above the limit (the 7-member case) keep existing members but can't add more until under the cap — no deletions.
2. **Idle auto-logout (10–15 min):** no idle tracking exists. Plan: frontend idle timer (activity events reset it; 15 min → logout + redirect to login with a "signed out due to inactivity" note), with a 1-minute warning toast. Backend token TTLs stay as they are (access tokens are already short-lived; the refresh cookie is what the timer effectively neutralizes).
3. **"Expiring_soon" label:** raw status codes render unformatted in the partner plan page and super-admin views. Plan: one shared status-label formatter (proper capitalization + spacing) applied everywhere a subscription/hotel status renders — this also covers several "first letter capital" marks.

## G. Polish batch (mechanical, low-risk)

Shift Handover label capitalization + 42px Create Handover button; plans page — center the "Current subscription" strip and plan CTA row, 42px styled buttons, "Valid until" capital; team-members action menu wider + "Edit Profile" capital; sidebar order Money → Staff (move STAFF group below MONEY per screenshot 7); "Use my current location" fix (needs repro — likely the browser permission error path breaks the layout; also requires HTTPS which production has); room-type-not-shown-after-add in Edit Hotel (new type likely missing from the rooms dropdown until refetch — same invalidation class as D); checkout Find Booking dropdown — exclude completed bookings, widen ~30%, single-line rows; capitalization sweep with a shared helper; vendor-detail popover on expenses rows (exists per screenshot 4 — verify deployed, fix trigger if dead).

---

## Sequencing (proposed)

1. **Phase 1 — Isolation & access (critical):** draft hotel-scoping + storage audit; expired-hotel server-side enforcement + overlay; production storage health verification (B2 env vars — this may resolve the photos cluster without code).
2. **Phase 2 — Check-in rework:** identity state machine, autofill (button + documents), draft schema v3 (co-guests + server-side doc persistence), same-day room rules, sync invalidation sweep.
3. **Phase 3 — Money:** auto-invoice at checkout, Edit Stay due, KPI cards ×3 screens, additional-charge details, booking number displays, Powered-by + toggle, GST repro.
4. **Phase 4 — Platform:** team limit, idle logout, cross-hotel guest search (pending question 2), status-label formatter.
5. **Phase 5 — Polish batch.**

Each phase ships with tsc/build/ruff/pytest + a manual verification list per issue, and every fix maps back to the client's item so you can hand the justification list to them.

---

## Questions before I implement anything

1. **Expired hotels:** completely block login, or allow login locked to the Plan/Renewal screen only (my strong recommendation — otherwise owners can't renew or see the extension you grant)?
2. **Cross-hotel guest search:** confirm guests should be searchable across **all hotels on the platform** by full phone / ID last-4, with full details + documents copied into the searching hotel only on explicit Autofill (audited). This is the one place data crosses hotels — I want explicit sign-off given the isolation work happening simultaneously.
3. **Existing drafts on devices:** after scoping, untagged old drafts get adopted by whichever hotel is active at first load on that device. Acceptable, or should old drafts be discarded entirely?
4. **Team limit of 5:** does the count include the owner, and do disabled members count? (My default: active members excluding owner.)
5. **Same-day check-in rooms:** physically occupied rooms — hard-block selection until the current guest checks out, or keep selectable with a blocking confirmation? (My default: hard-block; the "Free at 11:00" hint stays.)
6. **Draft documents:** OK to upload ID photos to the server at selection time (tied to the draft, auto-cleaned if discarded)? That's the only reliable way drafts can restore images.

Answer these (even briefly) and I'll start with Phase 1.
